$ DISCUSSION ON THE “SPECIAL DISCUSSION ON INDIA’S STRONG, 
SUCCESSFUL AND DECISIVE ‘OPERATION SINDOOR’ IN  
RESPONSE TO TERRORIST ATTACK IN PAHALGAM”–Contd. 
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN : Further discussion on In dia’s Strong, Successful and 
Decisive ‘Operation Sindoor’ in response to  Terrorist Attack in Pahalgam, raised by 
Shri Rajnath Singh, hon. Mi nister of Defence, on 29 th July, 2025. Now, 
Shri S. Jaishankar.  
THE MINISTER OF EXTERNAL AFFAIRS (SHRI S. JAISHANKAR) : Hon. Deputy 
Chairman, Sir, it is a privilege to participate in the discussion on Operation Sindoor, 
and, like everybody else, I laud the perfor mance of our Armed Forces and the results 
that have done this country proud. 
Sir, when the Pahalgam attack happened on April 22 nd, it was a shocking 
attack for a variety of reasons - the manner in which people were killed in front of their 
families, the fact that thei r faith was asked before killin g them and the intent of 
destroying the economy of Jammu and Kashmir, which had returned to normalcy and 
prosperity after the end of Article 370.  All of this an gered the country.  There was 
grief, there was shock.  Across the world, there was solidarity, there was sympathy. 
But the question was what after that, and the answers were two.  One, such an 
attack was absolutely unacceptable.  It crossed our red lines and there had to be 
severe consequences.  Two, there had to be accountability and justice, there had to 
be accountability of the perpetrators, and th ey and their supports had to be brought 
to justice. The first clear message of India’s anger, of our resolve, of our 
determination, was the decision taken by the Cabinet Committee on Security 
immediately the day after, that is, 23 rd April, 2025.  A number of steps were taken. 
$ Further discussion continued from the 29th July, 2025. 
12
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

Pakistani visas under SAARC were cancelle d, the Mission was reduced in strength 
and the Military Advisors were expelled but the most important decision was to hold 
the Indus Waters Treaty in abeyance.  There has been a lot of discussion in this 
House.  Various Members have expressed their opinion as they should  but, I think, it 
is necessary that the House understands and appreciates the importance of this 
decision.   
Sir, the Indus Waters Treaty in many ways is a very unique agreement.  I 
cannot think of any agreement in the world where a country has allowed its major 
rivers to flow to the next country without ha ving rights on those rivers.  It was an 
extraordinary agreement.  It is important, when we have put it in abeyance, to recall 
the history of this agreement.  Yest erday, I heard, some Members were 
uncomfortable with history. They prefer that historical things are forgotton -- maybe it 
does not suit them --  and they only like to recall some of the things.   So, let me, for 
the benefit of this House, mention three important observations which were made in 
Parliament on the issue of Indus Waters Tr eaty.  This was on November 30, 1960 and 
the observations were made by  the then Prime Minister on this subject.   First, he 
said, “I would like to know: Is this House now to judge the quantum of supply of water 
or the quantum of money to be given? I confess I cannot judge it offhand. ”  So, the 
Prime Minister of India then is telling th e Parliament, you canno t be a judge of the 
quantum of water. You should not be looking at how much money we are giving to 
Pakistan. That is not your business. Let me , Sir, give you a second quote. The Prime 
Minister then goes on, because many Member s objected to the Indus Waters Treaty, 
and he says, “Hon. Members have been saying that  there was a partition. What has 
happened is none of our lookout. We should not give them anything. ” Now, the then 
Prime Minister's view is, “That is neither a strictly legal, constitutional or just approach 
to this problem. If we follow that approach, it would mean turning a great part of West 
Punjab into almost a wilderness. ” West Punjab is Pakistani Punjab. So, the Prime 
Minister is saying, let us do  this Treaty because the Indian Parliament, the Indian 
Government, the country should put the interests of Pakistani Punjab.  Sir, not a word 
about the interests of the kisan of Kashmir, of the people of Punjab!

ﺭﮨﮯ ﮨﻴﮟ ﮨﻢ ﻧﮯ ﮐﺴﺘﺎﻥﭘﺎ ﭘﺮ ﺍﭨﻴﮏ ﻧﮩﻴﮟ ﮐﻴﺎ۔ ﮨﻢ ﻧﮯ ﺍﺱ ﮐﮯ ﺍﻧﺪﺭ ﺟﺎﮐﺮ ﺍﻥ ﮐﯽ ﺳﻮچ ﮐﻮ  ﻧﻴﺴﺖ ﻭ ﻧﺎﺑﻮﺩ ﮐﻴﺎ۔  ﺍﺱ ﻟﻴﮯ ﮨﻢ 
ﻧﮯ ﺍﻥ ﮐﻮ ﺑﺘﺎﻳﺎ ﺗﻬﺎ۔ ﺍﻥ ﮐﯽ ﺳﻴﻨﺎ ﮐﻮ ﻣﻌﻠﻮﻡ ﻧﮩﻴﮟ ﮨﻮﺍ۔  
  ﻭﻩ ﮐﮩہ ﺭﮨﮯ ﺗﻬﮯ ﮐہ ﮐﭙﻮﺍڑﻩ، ﺍڑی، ﭘﻨﭽﻬ، ﺭﺍﺟﻮﺭی، ﺟﻤﻮں ﻣﻴﮟ ﺍﻭﺭ ﭘﻨﺠﺎﺏ ﺳﮯ ﻟﻴﮑﺮ ﺟﻴﺴﻠﻤﻴﺮ ﺗﮏ ﻧﻘﺼﺎﻥ 
ﮨﻮﺍ۔ ﺑﻠﮑﻞ، ﺟﻨﮓ ﻣﻴﮟ ﻧﻘﺼﺎﻥ ﺗﺎﮨﻮ ﮨﯽ ﮨﮯ۔ ﭘﺎﮐﺴﺘﺎﻥ ﻧﮯ ﮨﻤﺎﺭے ﺍﺳﭩﻴﺒﻠﺸﻤﻨﭧ ﭘﺮ ﺍﭨﻴﮏ ﮐﺮﻧﮯ ﮐﯽ ﮐﻮﺷﺶ ﮐﯽ۔  ﻟﻴﮑﻦ 
ﮨﻤﺎﺭے ﺟﻮ ﺍﻧﮉی ﺟﻴﻨﺲ، ﮨﻤﺎﺭی ﻣﻠﭩﺮی ﮐﮯ ﺑﻨﺎﺋﮯ ﮨﻮﺋﮯ ﮨﺘﻬﻴﺎﺭ ﺗﻬﮯ۔  ﺍﻧﮩﻮں ﻧﮯ ﺍﻥ ﮐﻮ ﮨﻮﺍ ﻴﮟ
ﻣ ﻓﻴﻮﺯ ﮐﺮ ﺩﻳﺎ۔ ﻣﻴﮟ ڈﻳﻔﻨﺲ 
ﻣﻨﺴﭩﺮ ﺻﺎﺣﺐ ﮐﻮ ﻣﺒﺎﺭﮐﺒﺎﺩ ﺩﻭں ﮔﺎ ﮐہ ﭘﻮﺭے ﺍﻧﭩﺮﻧﻴﺸﻨﻞ ﺑﺎﺭڈﺭ ﺍﻭﺭ ﺍﻳﻞ ﺍﻭ ﺳﯽ ﭘﺮ ﺟﻮ ﺑﻨﮑﺮ ﺋﮯﺑﻨﺎ ﮔﺌﮯ ﮨﻴﮟ ﺍﻭﺭ ﮨﻤﺎﺭی

DR. SASMIT PATRA (Odisha):  Hon. Vice-Chairman, Sir, I place on record my 
deepest homage and heartfel t co
ndolences to the innocent 26 precious lives lost in 
the barbaric terrorist attack in Pahalgam .  On behalf of our leader and Party 
President, Shri Naveen Patnaik and our Party, the Biju Janata Dal, I rise to express 
our profound gratitude to th e true heroes of Operatio n Sindoor, the men and women 
of our Armed Forces. When the skies over  Pahalgam darkened with the cries of 
innocent lives lost in a cowardly terroris t attack, it was not words but action that 
defined India’s response. That action came in the form of Operation Sindoor.  A swift, 
precise and decisive military action execute d with brilliance, courage and unmatched 
professionalism. And who delivered that br illiance? Who executed that courage? It 
was our soldiers, sailors and air warriors wh o stepped into the eye of the storm and 
returned victorious without fanfare.  The Armed Forces did not only represent 
leadership. They embodied the silent force be hind India’s global military credibility. It 
was not luck but the meticulous coordinati on, satellite intelligence, loitering munitions 
and above all, human brilliance.  From the p ilots who soared into the skies, to the 
signal corps who maintained flawless co mmunication, to the engineers who mapped 
target grids, to the logistic corps who filled this operation silently in the background, 
every rank, every uniform, every heartbeat was part of a single symphony -  Operation 
Sindoor.  
Odisha and its four-and-a-half crore peop le have always stood firmly with the 
Armed Forces and upheld the highest tradition of patriotism and national service.  The 
commitment draws inspiration from the legacy of  late Shri Biju Patnaik, a daring pilot 
and a freedom fighter whose he roic acts during the times of conflict, including his 
audacious rescue missions during the Indone sian freedom struggle and his role in the 
Kashmir conflict in 1947 among many others, re main etched in the annals of history.  
His courage, vision and deep nationalistic spirit continues to guide Odisha’s 
commitment to the nation and its defenders.   
However, hon. Vice-Chairman, Sir, I wi sh to respectfully place one important 
question before the Government, a matter that may warrant clarification by the 
Government.  The Government has stated th at there was no phone call made by the

President of the United States to the In dian Prime Minister between April 22nd and
